## GHSA-9x44-4gxf-8c25 · pimcore/pimcore

2026-08-28 · PHP · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient validation of user-supplied field names for DataObject class definitions. These field names were directly incorporated into generated PHP class files (as properties, getters/setters, and constants) and used verbatim in SQL ALTER TABLE DDL statements without proper sanitization or quoting. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary PHP code or SQL commands by crafting a malicious field name.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution on the server by injecting PHP code into the generated class files, or execute arbitrary SQL commands, leading to full system compromise, data manipulation, or data exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/models/DataObject/ClassDefinition/Data.php
+++ b/models/DataObject/ClassDefinition/Data.php
@@ -166,6 +167,14 @@ public function getPermissions(): array|string|null
      */
     public function setName(string $name): static
     {
+        if ($name !== '' && !preg_match('/^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]{0,62}$/', $name)) {
+            throw new InvalidArgumentException(sprintf('Invalid field name "%s"', $name));
+        }
+
         $this->name = $name;
 
         return $this;
--- a/models/DataObject/ClassDefinition/Helper/Dao.php
+++ b/models/DataObject/ClassDefinition/Helper/Dao.php
@@ -39,31 +39,31 @@ protected function addIndexToField(DataObject
                     // multicolumn field
                     foreach ($columnType as $fkey => $fvalue) {
                         $indexName = $field->getName().'__'.$fkey;
-                        $columnName = '`' . $indexName . '`';
+                        $columnName = $this->db->quoteIdentifier($indexName);
                         if ($unique) {
                             if ($isLocalized) {
-                                $columnName .= ',`language`';
+                                $columnName .= ',' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier('language');
                             } elseif ($isFieldcollection) {
-                                $columnName .= ',`fieldname`';
+                                $columnName .= ',' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier('fieldname');
                             }
                         }
                         if ($this->indexDoesNotExist($table, $prefix, $indexName)) {
-                            $this->db->executeQuery('ALTER TABLE `' . $table . '` ADD ' . $uniqueStr . 'INDEX `' . $prefix . $indexName . '` (' . $columnName . ');');
+                            $this->db->executeQuery('ALTER TABLE ' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier($table) . ' ADD ' . $uniqueStr . 'INDEX ' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier($prefix . $indexName) . ' (' . $columnName . ');');
                         }
                     }
                 } else {
                     // single -column field
                     $indexName = $field->getName();
-                    $columnName = '`' . $indexName . '`';
+                    $columnName = $this->db->quoteIdentifier($indexName);
                     if ($unique) {
                         if ($isLocalized) {
-                            $columnName .= ',`language`';
+                            $columnName .= ',' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier('language');
                         } elseif ($isFieldcollection) {
-                            $columnName .= ',`fieldname`';
+                            $columnName .= ',' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier('fieldname');
                         }
                     }
                     if ($this->indexDoesNotExist($table, $prefix, $indexName)) {
-                        $this->db->executeQuery('ALTER TABLE `' . $table . '` ADD ' . $uniqueStr . 'INDEX `' . $prefix . $indexName . '` (' . $columnName . ');');
+                        $this->db->executeQuery('ALTER TABLE ' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier($table) . ' ADD ' . $uniqueStr . 'INDEX ' . $this->db->quoteIdentifier($prefix . $indexName) . ' (' . $columnName . ');');
                     }
                 }
             } else {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a regular expression validation for DataObject field names to ensure they adhere to a strict alphanumeric and underscore format, preventing injection of special characters. Additionally, all SQL identifiers (table names, column names, index names) in ALTER TABLE statements are now properly quoted using `db->quoteIdentifier()` to prevent SQL injection.

## GHSA-c64q-hj4j-375f · org.yamcs:yamcs-core

2026-08-28 · Java · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The Yamcs StreamSQL `LIKE` expression directly embedded user-controlled pattern strings into dynamically compiled Java code (via Janino) without proper escaping. This allowed an authenticated attacker to inject arbitrary Java code into the `LikeExpression`'s `fillCode_getValueReturn` method.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could execute arbitrary code on the server, leading to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/yamcs-core/src/main/java/org/yamcs/yarch/streamsql/LikeExpression.java
+++ b/yamcs-core/src/main/java/org/yamcs/yarch/streamsql/LikeExpression.java
@@ -23,5 +23,5 @@ public void fillCode_getValueReturn(StringBuilder code) throws StreamSqlExceptio
         code.append("org.yamcs.yarch.streamsql.Utils.like(");
         children[0].fillCode_getValueReturn(code);
         code.append(", \"");
-        code.append(likeClause.pattern);
+        ValueExpression.escapeJavaString(likeClause.pattern, code);
         code.append("\")");
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a static `escapeJavaString` method in `ValueExpression` and applies it to the `likeClause.pattern` before embedding it into the dynamically generated Java code. This ensures that special characters in the user-provided pattern are properly escaped, preventing code injection.

## GHSA-73mf-m39p-wpm9 · org.yamcs:yamcs-core

2026-08-28 · Java · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the Yamcs server processing user-controlled input as part of an instance-template argument, which was then directly fed into a YAML parser. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary YAML, including directives that could lead to object instantiation and method invocation, effectively achieving Remote Code Execution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the server running Yamcs, leading to full compromise of the system, including data theft, modification, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/yamcs-core/src/main/java/org/yamcs/templating/Template.java
+++ b/yamcs-core/src/main/java/org/yamcs/templating/Template.java
@@ -47,6 +52,66 @@ public String process(Map<String, Object> args) {
         return templateProcessor.process(args);
     }
 
+    public String processAndSanitizeYaml(Map<String, Object> userArgs) {
+        Map<String, Object> tokenArgs = new HashMap<>();
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a sanitization layer for YAML processing. It replaces user-provided string arguments with unique UUID tokens before template processing. After the template is rendered, the output is parsed into a YAML object structure, and then the tokens are safely replaced with their original string values. This ensures that user input is treated as data and not as executable YAML directives.

## GHSA-cgc5-v3f2-8m2v · github.com/klever-io/klever-go

2026-08-28 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `INTEGER_OVERFLOW→BOUNDARY`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an integer overflow in the `SFTAddCirculation` function when calculating the total circulation of KLV tokens. When a large `amount` was added to `meta.Circulation`, if the sum exceeded the maximum value for the `int64` data type, it would wrap around to a negative value. This made it possible for an attacker to bypass the `meta.Circulation > meta.MaxSupply` check, as the overflowed `meta.Circulation` would appear smaller than `meta.MaxSupply` or even negative, allowing for unbounded minting.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this integer overflow to mint an arbitrary, unbounded amount of KLV tokens, effectively creating an infinite supply. This would severely devalue the KLV token, disrupt the economic stability of the Klever blockchain, and potentially lead to a complete loss of trust and value for the cryptocurrency.

### Key diff

```
--- a/core/kapp/systemAccount/systemAcount.go
+++ b/core/kapp/systemAccount/systemAcount.go
@@ -129,10 +129,15 @@ func (s *systemAccountKApp) SFTAddCirculation(asset, nonce []byte, amount int64)
 		return err
 	}
 
+	previousCirculation := meta.Circulation
 	meta.Circulation += amount
 
 	log.Trace("SFTAddCirculation", "max supply", meta.MaxSupply, "value", meta.Circulation)
 
+	if s.forkController.FixMarketBuyOverflow() && amount > 0 && meta.Circulation < previousCirculation {
+		return common.ErrMaxSupplyExceeded
+	}
+
 	if meta.Circulation > meta.MaxSupply && meta.MaxSupply != 0 {
 		return common.ErrMaxSupplyExceeded
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check in `SFTAddCirculation` to detect integer overflow before the `meta.Circulation > meta.MaxSupply` check. Specifically, it verifies that if `amount` is positive, the new `meta.Circulation` must not be less than the `previousCirculation`. Additionally, the patch freezes a set of accounts identified as being involved in the exploit, preventing them from submitting or relaying transactions, and ensures that block proposers skip transactions from these frozen accounts.

## GHSA-p7gw-2pcp-5pf8 · github.com/klever-io/klever-go

2026-08-28 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `INTEGER_OVERFLOW→BOUNDARY`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an integer overflow in the marketplace settlement logic. When the sum of referral and royalty percentages exceeded the bid amount, the seller's share would become negative. This negative value was silently skipped, leading to an incorrect calculation that allowed the minting of new KLV tokens, effectively creating value out of thin air.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this flaw to mint an arbitrary amount of KLV tokens, leading to an unauthorized increase in the cryptocurrency supply and potentially devaluing the currency for legitimate holders. This constitutes a severe economic impact on the blockchain ecosystem.

### Key diff

```
--- core/kapp/systemAccount/systemAcount.go
+++ core/kapp/systemAccount/systemAcount.go
@@ -129,10 +129,15 @@ func (s *systemAccountKApp) SFTAddCirculation(asset, nonce []byte, amount int64)
 		return err
 	}
 
+	previousCirculation := meta.Circulation
 	meta.Circulation += amount
 
 	log.Trace("SFTAddCirculation", "max supply", meta.MaxSupply, "value", meta.Circulation)
 
+	if s.forkController.FixMarketBuyOverflow() && amount > 0 && meta.Circulation < previousCirculation {
+		return common.ErrMaxSupplyExceeded
+	}
+
 	if meta.Circulation > meta.MaxSupply && meta.MaxSupply != 0 {
 		return common.ErrMaxSupplyExceeded
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `frozenAccounts` mechanism to block transactions from identified malicious accounts. It also adds checks in `SFTAddCirculation` to detect and prevent integer overflows when updating token circulation, specifically checking if `meta.Circulation` becomes less than `previousCirculation` after adding a positive `amount`. Additionally, transaction processing and block creation now explicitly skip transactions originating from frozen accounts.

## GHSA-3g44-3m7x-cgg2 · org.yamcs:yamcs-core

2026-08-28 · Java · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient input validation in the StreamSQL parser, specifically within the `S_DOUBLE_QUOTED_IDENTIFIER` token definition. Previously, this token allowed almost any character inside double quotes, including those that could be interpreted as code or commands by the aggregate compiler. This permitted an authenticated attacker to inject malicious code into column names.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) by injecting arbitrary code into StreamSQL queries, leading to full compromise of the Yamcs server.

### Key diff

```
--- a/yamcs-core/src/main/java/org/yamcs/yarch/streamsql/StreamSql.jj
+++ b/yamcs-core/src/main/java/org/yamcs/yarch/streamsql/StreamSql.jj
-  | < S_DOUBLE_QUOTED_IDENTIFIER: "\"" (~["\n","\r","\""])* "\"" >
+  | < S_DOUBLE_QUOTED_IDENTIFIER: "\"" (<LETTER> | <DIGIT> | <SPECIAL_CHARS>)+ "\"" >
```

### Fix summary

The patch restricts the characters allowed within double-quoted identifiers (`S_DOUBLE_QUOTED_IDENTIFIER`) to only letters, digits, and specific safe special characters ($, _, #, .). This prevents the injection of arbitrary code into column names, thereby mitigating the RCE vulnerability.

## GHSA-x5g3-w747-2h8q · plone.app.portlets

2026-08-28 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application allowed an attacker to specify an arbitrary URL for an RSS feed. Previously, it only performed a basic check to ensure the scheme was 'http' or 'https' and that the URL didn't contain multiple lines. This insufficient validation allowed an attacker to provide URLs that could lead to internal resource requests or other malicious actions, potentially causing the server to hang or consume excessive resources.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted RSS feed URL that points to internal network resources or a non-responsive server, leading to a denial of service for the Plone instance as it attempts to fetch the malicious feed, consuming resources and potentially hanging.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/plone/app/portlets/portlets/rss.py
+++ b/src/plone/app/portlets/portlets/rss.py
@@ -154,11 +266,11 @@ def _retrieveFeed(self):
         """do the actual work and try to retrieve the feed"""
         url = self.url
         if url:
-            if len(url.splitlines()) > 1:
-                # More than one line in a url: probably a hacker.
-                url = ""
-            elif urlparse(url).scheme not in ("https", "http"):
-                # Mostly: prevent loading local file: urls.
+            # Prevent loading local file: urls and other possible hacks.
+            try:
+                _rss_feed_url_validator(url)
+            except Invalid:
+                logger.warning("Refusing to load stored RSS url %r", url)
                 url = ""
         if url != "":
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a robust URL validator, `_rss_feed_url_validator`, which performs comprehensive checks on the provided RSS feed URL. It ensures the URL uses only 'http' or 'https' schemes, disallows IP addresses, internal hostnames (like 'localhost' or 'host.docker.internal'), and prevents port scanning. This validator is applied both during the initial configuration of the portlet and when the feed is retrieved.

---

## GHSA-gg93-x632-9ccv · code.vikunja.io/api

2026-08-28 · Go · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application's authorization checks for deleting and updating project views only verified if the user was an administrator of the associated project, but failed to confirm that the specific project view being acted upon actually belonged to the project specified in the request path. This allowed an attacker to manipulate the `ProjectID` in the request to bypass authorization checks for views belonging to other projects.

### Impact

An attacker could delete or update project views belonging to other users or projects without proper authorization, leading to data manipulation or denial of service for those views.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/models/project_view_permissions.go
+++ b/pkg/models/project_view_permissions.go
@@ -45,6 +45,10 @@ func (pv *ProjectView) CanDelete(s *xorm.Session, a web.Auth) (bool, error) {
 		return sf.CanDelete(s, a)
 	}
 
+	if _, err := GetProjectViewByIDAndProject(s, pv.ID, pv.ProjectID); err != nil {
+		return false, err
+	}
+
 	pp := pv.getProject()
 	return pp.IsAdmin(s, a)
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an explicit check in the `CanDelete` and `CanUpdate` methods to ensure that the `ProjectView` being operated on actually exists and belongs to the `ProjectID` specified in the request. This prevents an attacker from referencing a view from one project while providing a different `ProjectID` to bypass authorization.

## GHSA-jw39-3688-r4rx · compliance-trestle

2026-08-28 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The application was re-parsing untrusted user-supplied content (e.g., OSCAL data, markdown sections) using a standard Jinja2 environment. This allowed an attacker to embed Jinja2 template syntax within the untrusted data, which would then be evaluated by the server, leading to arbitrary code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the server by injecting malicious Jinja2 template syntax into data that is later re-evaluated as a template. This could lead to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/trestle/core/jinja/tags.py
+++ b/trestle/core/jinja/tags.py
@@ -97,7 +104,19 @@ def parse(self, parser):
                 f'Unable to retrieve section "{section_title.value}"" from {markdown_source.value} jinja template.'
             )
-        local_parser = Parser(self.environment, md_section.content.raw_text)
+        # SECURITY FIX: Use sandboxed environment for re-parsing to prevent SSTI
+        # Create a sandboxed environment if the current one isn't already sandboxed
+        parse_env = self.environment
+        if not isinstance(self.environment, SandboxedEnvironment):
+            parse_env = SandboxedEnvironment(
+                loader=self.environment.loader,
+                extensions=self.environment.extensions,
+                trim_blocks=self.environment.trim_blocks,
+                autoescape=self.environment.autoescape,
+            )
+
+        local_parser = Parser(parse_env, md_section.content.raw_text)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the use of Jinja2's SandboxedEnvironment for re-parsing untrusted content, which restricts access to unsafe Python attributes and prevents arbitrary code execution. Additionally, a defense-in-depth measure was added to neutralize Jinja2 delimiters ({{, }}) in untrusted prose and descriptions before they are written to markdown, further mitigating the risk of template injection.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 13 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Python, JavaScript
> → GHSA-xj4f-8jjg-vx4q
> → GHSA-x7m9-mwc2-g6w2

## GHSA-575r-357h-fhch · snipe/snipe-it

2026-08-28 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application's API endpoint for updating asset maintenance records only checked if the current user had access to the *existing* asset associated with the maintenance. It failed to perform an authorization check on a *new* asset_id provided in the request, allowing a user to re-parent a maintenance record to an asset belonging to a different company.

### Impact

An attacker could move asset maintenance records between different companies without proper authorization, potentially leading to data manipulation or denial of service for legitimate users by associating their maintenance records with assets they don't own.

### Key diff

```
--- a/app/Http/Controllers/Api/MaintenancesController.php
+++ b/app/Http/Controllers/Api/MaintenancesController.php
@@ -274,6 +274,18 @@ public function update(Request $request, $id): JsonResponse|array
             if (! Company::isCurrentUserHasAccess($maintenance->asset)) {
                 return response()->json(Helper::formatStandardApiResponse('error', null, trans('general.action_permission_denied', ['item_type' => trans('admin/maintenances/general.maintenance'), 'id' => $id, 'action' => trans('general.edit')])));
             }
+
+            // If the request changes asset_id, verify the new asset is accessible
+            if ($request->filled('asset_id') && (int) $request->input('asset_id') !== $maintenance->asset_id) {
+                $newAsset = Asset::find($request->input('asset_id'));
+                if (! Company::isCurrentUserHasAccess($newAsset)) {
+                    return response()->json(Helper::formatStandardApiResponse('error', null, trans('general.action_permission_denied', ['item_type' => trans('general.asset'), 'id' => $request->input('asset_id'), 'action' => trans('general.edit')])), 403);
+                }
+                $maintenance->fill($request->except('asset_id'));
+                $maintenance->asset_id = $newAsset->id;
+            } else {
+                $maintenance->fill($request->except('asset_id'));
+            }
 
-            $maintenance->fill($request->all());
             if ($maintenance->save()) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an explicit authorization check when the `asset_id` field is updated. It now verifies that the new asset specified in the request also belongs to a company the current user has access to, preventing cross-company re-parenting of maintenance records.

## GHSA-9jg3-g3wh-w9pj · org.yamcs:yamcs-core

2026-08-28 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed unauthenticated users to request static files. The `locateFile` method did not properly sanitize or validate user-supplied paths, allowing directory traversal sequences (e.g., `../`) to escape the intended static root directories.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could read arbitrary files on the server's file system that are accessible by the Yamcs process, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
-            var file = staticRoot.resolve(path).toFile();
-            if (!file.isHidden() && file.exists()) {
-                return file;
+            var path = staticRoot.resolve(pathString);
+            if (path.normalize().toAbsolutePath().startsWith(staticRoot.normalize().toAbsolutePath())) {
+                var file = path.toFile();
+                if (!file.isHidden() && file.exists()) {
+                    return file;
+                }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a normalization and absolute path check to ensure that the resolved file path always remains within the defined static root directory. It explicitly checks if the normalized absolute path of the requested file starts with the normalized absolute path of the static root.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 75 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript, Java, PHP, C#, Go, RUST, Ruby

## GHSA-q79r-r9xg-r863 · org.graylog2:graylog2-server

2026-08-28 · Java · **MODERATE** 5.0 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the system catalog titles endpoint, when used with composite display fields, did not adequately check if all requested fields were readable by the current user. While a general read permission check was performed, it overlooked the specific field-level permissions for composite displays, allowing an attacker to retrieve values from protected database fields.

### Impact

An attacker with access to the system catalog titles endpoint could retrieve sensitive information from database fields that they should not have permission to read, potentially leading to the exposure of confidential data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/graylog2-server/src/main/java/org/graylog2/rest/resources/system/contentpacks/titles/EntityTitleServiceImpl.java
+++ b/graylog2-server/src/main/java/org/graylog2/rest/resources/system/contentpacks/titles/EntityTitleServiceImpl.java
@@ -153,7 +157,8 @@ private EntitiesTitleResponse getTitlesForEntitiesFromSingleCollection(final Ent
                     return;
                 }
 
-                final boolean canReadTitle = checkCanReadTitle(permissions, dbEntityCatalogEntry.get().readPermission(), idAsString);
+                final boolean canReadTitle = checkCanReadTitle(permissions, dbEntityCatalogEntry.get().readPermission(), idAsString)
+                        && (!useCompositeDisplay || entityPermissionsUtils.areAllFieldsReadable(collection, displayFields));
                 final String title;
                 if (!canReadTitle) {
                     title = TITLE_IF_NOT_PERMITTED;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an additional permission check to ensure that all fields requested for a composite display are readable by the current user. It injects `EntityPermissionsUtils` and adds a condition `entityPermissionsUtils.areAllFieldsReadable(collection, displayFields)` to the `canReadTitle` boolean, preventing the disclosure of protected field values.

## GHSA-53jc-27pc-x8r8 · snipe/snipe-it

2026-08-28 · PHP · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application allowed any authenticated user to cancel an asset request by setting the `cancel_by_admin` parameter to true, without properly checking if the user had administrative privileges. The authorization check `this->authorize('index', Asset::class)` was insufficient as it only checked for general asset viewing permissions, not specific administrative actions like canceling requests on behalf of others.

### Impact

An authenticated, non-administrative user could cancel asset requests made by other users, leading to denial of service for legitimate asset requests and potential disruption of asset management workflows.

### Key diff

```
--- a/app/Http/Controllers/ViewAssetsController.php
+++ b/app/Http/Controllers/ViewAssetsController.php
@@ -199,17 +200,23 @@ public function getRequestItem(Request $request, $itemType, $itemId = null, $can
 
         $settings = Setting::getSettings();
 
-        if ($cancel_by_admin) {
-            $this->authorize('index', Asset::class);
+        $is_admin = $user->isSuperUser() || $user->isAdmin();
+
+        if ($cancel_by_admin && ! $is_admin) {
+            return redirect()->back()->with('error', trans('general.insufficient_permissions'));
         }
 
-        if (($item_request = $item->isRequestedBy($user)) || $cancel_by_admin) {
-            $item->cancelRequest($cancel_by_admin ? $requestingUser : null);
+        if (($item_request = $item->isRequestedBy($user)) || ($is_admin && $cancel_by_admin)) {
+            $item->cancelRequest($is_admin && $cancel_by_admin ? $requestingUser : null);
             $data['item_quantity'] = ($item_request) ? $item_request->qty : 1;
             $logaction->logaction(ActionType::RequestCanceled);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an explicit check for administrative privileges (`$user->isSuperUser() || $user->isAdmin()`) before allowing the `cancel_by_admin` functionality. If a non-admin user attempts to use this parameter, they are redirected with an insufficient permissions error. Additionally, the logic for calling `cancelRequest` was updated to only pass the requesting user for cancellation if the action is performed by an administrator.

## GHSA-8jqh-95g6-7jpj · phalcon/cphalcon

2026-08-28 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `TIMING_SIDE_CHANNEL→TOKEN_LEAK`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the HMAC verification in the `decrypt` method used a non-constant-time comparison function (`ZEPHIR_IS_IDENTICAL`). This allowed an attacker to potentially deduce information about the HMAC digest by measuring the time taken for the comparison, leading to a timing side-channel attack.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially use timing differences during HMAC verification to reconstruct the valid HMAC digest, thereby bypassing integrity checks and potentially forging encrypted messages without knowing the encryption key.

### Key diff

```
- if (!ZEPHIR_IS_IDENTICAL(&digest, &_11$$8)) {
+ if (1 != zephir_hash_equals(&_11$$8, &digest)) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the non-constant-time `ZEPHIR_IS_IDENTICAL` comparison with `zephir_hash_equals` for HMAC verification. This new function performs a constant-time comparison, mitigating the timing side-channel vulnerability.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 6 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, PHP, Java, Python
> → GHSA-jj6q-rrrf-h66h
> → GHSA-r854-jrxh-36qx
> → GHSA-xmj9-7625-f634

> → GHSA-g6cj-pr64-35w5

## GHSA-p6gw-4frg-j7jw · WsgiDAV

2026-08-28 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The application constructed SQL queries by directly concatenating user-controlled input (like table names and primary key values) into the SQL string without proper escaping or parameterization. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary SQL code.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary SQL commands on the underlying MySQL database, potentially leading to data exfiltration, modification, or deletion, and possibly remote code execution depending on the database configuration and privileges.

### Key diff

```
--- a/wsgidav/samples/mysql_dav_provider.py
+++ b/wsgidav/samples/mysql_dav_provider.py
@@ -371,49 +382,26 @@ def _is_data_type_numeric(self, datatype):
 
     def _exists_record_by_primary_key(self, conn, table_name, pri_key_value):
         pri_key = None
-        pri_field_type = None
         cursor = conn.cursor(MySQLdb.cursors.DictCursor)
-        cursor.execute("DESCRIBE " + table_name)
+        cursor.execute("DESCRIBE `%s`" % _escape_id(table_name))
         result_set = cursor.fetchall()
         for row in result_set:
             if row["Key"] == "PRI":
                 if pri_key is None:
                     pri_key = row["Field"]
-                    pri_field_type = row["Type"]
                 else:
                     return False  # more than one primary key - multipart key?
         cursor.close()
 
-        isNumType = self._is_data_type_numeric(pri_field_type)
-
         cursor = conn.cursor(MySQLdb.cursors.DictCursor)
-        if isNumType:
-            cursor.execute(
-                "SELECT "
-                + pri_key
-                + " FROM "
-                + self._db
-                + "."
-                + table_name
-                + " WHERE "
-                + pri_key
-                + " = "
-                + pri_key_value
-            )
-        else:
-            cursor.execute(
-                "SELECT "
-                + pri_key
-                + " FROM "
-                + self._db
-                + "."
-                + table_name
-                + " WHERE "
-                + pri_key
-                + " = '"
-                + pri_key_value
-                + "'"
-            )
+        sql = (
+            "SELECT `{pri_key}` FROM `{db}`.`{table}` WHERE `{pri_key}` = %s"
+        ).format(
+            pri_key=_escape_id(pri_key),
+            db=_escape_id(self._db),
+            table=_escape_id(table_name),
+        )
+        cursor.execute(sql, (pri_key_value,))
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `_escape_id` function to properly escape MySQL identifiers (table/column names) and switches from string concatenation to parameterized queries (using `%s` placeholders) for user-controlled values in `WHERE` clauses, preventing SQL injection.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 29 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, PHP, JavaScript, Ruby, Java, C#, Erlang, Python

> → GHSA-875v-7m49-8x88

## GHSA-w23p-wrp7-ch38 · pimcore/pimcore

2026-08-28 · PHP · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→OBJECT_INJECTION`

### Root cause

The application was using `Serialize::unserialize()` on user-controlled data retrieved from the object-store column without restricting the classes that could be deserialized. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary PHP objects.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution (RCE) by crafting a malicious serialized object that, upon deserialization, would trigger dangerous magic methods in existing classes.

### Key diff

```
--- a/models/DataObject/ClassDefinition/Data/Hotspotimage.php
+++ b/models/DataObject/ClassDefinition/Data/Hotspotimage.php
@@ -128,7 +128,7 @@ public function getDataFromResource(mixed $data, ?DataObject
             // check if the data is JSON (backward compatibility)
             $md = json_decode($metaData, true);
             if (!$md) {
-                $md = Serialize::unserialize($metaData);
+                $md = Serialize::unserialize($metaData, [Element\Data\MarkerHotspotItem::class]);
             } elseif (is_array($md)) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch restricts the classes that can be deserialized by passing an array of allowed classes to the `Serialize::unserialize()` method. This ensures that only expected and safe objects can be created from the serialized data.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 5 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, JavaScript
> → GHSA-rmj7-2vxq-3g9f
> → GHSA-j3rv-43j4-c7qm
> → GHSA-9369-69wj-7m2f
> → GHSA-337j-9hxr-rhxg

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/pimcore/pimcore/commit/b184c01bf11e213e601d965b4e96c8bb7248e980)

## GHSA-x7rj-f32v-7jjg · phalcon/cphalcon

2026-08-28 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the use of a regular expression with a catastrophic backtracking pattern, specifically `(/.*)*`, in the default Phalcon Router routes. This pattern allowed for an exponential increase in processing time for certain malicious input strings.

### Impact

An unauthenticated remote attacker could send a specially crafted URL to the application, causing the server to spend an excessive amount of time processing the regular expression, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/phalcon/Mvc/Router.zep
+++ b/phalcon/Mvc/Router.zep
@@ -346,7 +346,7 @@ class Router extends AbstractInjectionAware implements RouterInterface, EventsAw
 
             this->attach(
                 new Route(
-                    "#^/([\\w0-9\\_\\-]+)/([\\w0-9\\.\\_]+)(/.*)*$#u",
+                    "#^/([\\w0-9\\_\\-]+)/([\\w0-9\\.\\_]+)(/.*)?$#u",
                     [
                         "controller": 1,
                         "action":     2,
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the problematic regular expression patterns in the Phalcon CLI and MVC routers. It changes the quantifier from `(/.*)*` to `(/.*)?`, which prevents catastrophic backtracking by limiting the repetition of the `.*` group to at most one occurrence.

## GHSA-x8mj-6p3q-g5pp · github.com/free5gc/free5gc

2026-08-28 · Go · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The free5GC NRF component lacked comprehensive input validation for NF Profile data during registration and update operations. Specifically, it only validated the 'NfInstanceId', 'NfType', and 'NfStatus' fields, allowing attackers to inject arbitrary values into other critical fields like 'NfServices' and 'IpEndPoints' by manipulating the raw JSON payload.

### Impact

An attacker could register or update a Network Function (NF) with a poisoned profile, including arbitrary service endpoints. This could lead to other network functions connecting to attacker-controlled services, enabling man-in-the-middle attacks, data interception, or redirection of legitimate 5G traffic.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/sbi/processor/nf_management.go
+++ b/internal/sbi/processor/nf_management.go
@@ -485,9 +551,20 @@ func (p *Processor) GetNFInstanceProcedure(c *gin.Context, nfInstanceID string)
 func (p *Processor) NFRegisterProcedure(
 	c *gin.Context,
 	nfProfile *models.NrfNfManagementNfProfile,
+	rawProfile []byte,
 ) {
 	logger.NfmLog.Traceln("[NRF] In NFRegisterProcedure")
 
+	if err := validateNfProfileJSON(rawProfile, nfProfile); err != nil {
+		problemDetails := &models.ProblemDetails{
+			Title:  "Malformed request syntax",
+			Status: http.StatusBadRequest,
+			Detail: err.Error(),
+		}
+		util.GinProblemJson(c, problemDetails)
+		return
+	}
+
 	if nfProfile.NfInstanceId == "" || nfProfile.NfType == "" || nfProfile.NfStatus == "" {
 		problemDetails := &models.ProblemDetails{
 			Title:  "Mandatory IE missing",
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `nf_profile_validation.go` file with extensive validation logic for NF profiles. It now validates the raw JSON input and the unmarshalled `NrfNfManagementNfProfile` object, checking fields like `heartBeatTimer`, `ipv4Addresses`, `ipv6Addresses`, and `nfServices` (including their schemes, statuses, and IP endpoints) to ensure they conform to expected formats and ranges.
